Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\n(this is the start of the consultation)\n\n[Last Question]\nHello, I’m Dr. ____. What brings you in today?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 46 / no_occupation / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Weight_Appetite_Change\nmanifestation: \"What finally pushed me to come in is that my eating has been all over the place—some weeks I barely touch dinner, and other nights I stand in the kitchen eating crackers and sweets after everyone’s gone to bed.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, Doctor. Well, um, what finally pushed me to come in is that my eating has been all over the place lately. Like, some weeks I barely touch dinner, you know? And then other nights, I just stand in the kitchen eating crackers and sweets after everyone’s gone to bed. It’s so quiet then. I used to love the quiet when I lived in my old apartment—it was on the third floor, and you could hear the traffic, but it was like a hum, almost like the ocean. I went to the beach once when I was in college, just drove down with some friends on a whim. We didn't even pack towels. Anyway, yeah, my appetite is just completely unpredictable right now.

Oh, goodness, no. I've never done anything like that. I absolutely hate throwing up. I think the last time I was even sick like that was when I got terrible motion sickness. My husband and I went on this ferry ride a few years back to visit this little island off the coast, and the water was just so choppy that day, all gray and angry looking. I usually love the water, though. We used to go to the community pool all the time when I was a kid, and I'd just float on my back and look up at the clouds. You can really see shapes in them if you stare long enough. I saw one that looked exactly like a golden retriever once. We had a golden retriever named Buster when I was little, and he was just the sweetest thing. He used to sleep right at the foot of my bed and keep my feet warm in the winter. Dogs are just so loyal, aren't they? They just want to be near you. But, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, throwing up. No, I definitely don't make myself do that.

Um, weight? Well, what finally pushed me to come in is that my eating has been all over the place lately. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and other nights I just stand in the kitchen eating crackers and sweets after everyone's gone to bed. The kitchen gets so quiet at night, you know? We'd play capture the flag with all the neighborhood kids, and my knees were always covered in grass stains. My mother hated trying to get those stains out of my jeans. She had this special soap she ordered from a catalog that smelled like pine needles. I love the smell of pine, actually. It reminds me of Christmas. Oh, weight. I haven't really been weighing myself, but my eating is definitely just... all over the place.

Anyway, yeah, my appetite is just completely unpredictable right now.\n\nDoctor: When you eat those crackers and sweets at night, do you feel like you eat an unusually large amount of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say it's a ton of food all at once or anything like that. Just a normal amount, maybe a handful of cookies or a few crackers. I usually get those little lemon cookies, the ones with the powdered sugar on them. My grandmother used to bake lemon cookies from scratch, actually. She had this heavy old yellow ceramic bowl she mixed everything in, and she always let me lick the spoon. She lived way out in the country, and the stars out there were just incredible. You really can't see stars like that anymore because of all the light pollution. I read this fascinating article the other day about how light pollution is completely messing up bird migrations. They get so confused by the city lights... it’s really sad for them, honestly, just flying around lost. But, um, what was I saying? Oh, right. No, I don't gorge myself or eat super fast. Just regular snacking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a lack of interest in sex?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I haven't really noticed any changes there. Things are just... normal, I guess. I mean, my husband and I have been married for fifteen years, so it's not like it was when we first met. We met at this little coffee shop downtown, actually. It's closed now, which is a shame because they had the best blueberry scones. Real blueberries, not those fake little chewy bits you get in the muffins at the grocery store. I always try to buy local produce when I can, you know? There's this farmer's market on Saturdays, and last week I bought these gorgeous heirloom tomatoes. They were so red and juicy, I just sliced them up with some mozzarella and basil. Basil is so easy to grow, I should really start a little herb garden on the windowsill. The sunlight hits the kitchen window perfectly in the mornings. But, um, yeah, sex drive is fine. No issues there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I've never done anything like that. I absolutely hate throwing up. I think the last time I was even sick like that was when I got terrible motion sickness. My husband and I went on this ferry ride a few years back to visit this little island off the coast, and the water was just so choppy that day, all gray and angry looking. I usually love the water, though. We used to go to the community pool all the time when I was a kid, and I'd just float on my back and look up at the clouds. You can really see shapes in them if you stare long enough. I saw one that looked exactly like a golden retriever once. We had a golden retriever named Buster when I was little, and he was just the sweetest thing. He used to sleep right at the foot of my bed and keep my feet warm in the winter. Dogs are just so loyal, aren't they? They just want to be near you. But, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, throwing up. No, I definitely don't make myself do that.\n\nDoctor: When you eat like that at night, do you feel out of control?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not out of control at all. It's really just a little snack while I'm sitting there watching TV. I usually watch those old sitcom reruns on that classic channel, you know? Though sometimes they just play those crazy infomercials if it gets late enough. The other night there was one for this incredible blender that can supposedly turn whole apples and carrots into juice in like ten seconds. My mom used to have this awful, heavy green blender back in the 80s that sounded exactly like a jet engine taking off every time she made margaritas. She loved having the neighbors over for margaritas on the back patio during the summer. We had this massive oak tree in the backyard that gave the absolute best shade, at least until lightning struck a huge branch off it during a summer storm. The thunder was so loud it shook the whole house and rattled all her good china in the dining room cabinet. I was terrified of storms as a kid. But, um... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, being out of control with the food. No, no, it's nothing like that. I can stop eating whenever I want to.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a lot of weight recently, or are you currently underweight?\n\nPatient: Um, weight? Well, what finally pushed me to come in is that my eating has been all over the place lately. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and other nights I just stand in the kitchen eating crackers and sweets after everyone's gone to bed. The kitchen gets so quiet at night, you know? You can hear the hum of the refrigerator. We had to get a new refrigerator last year because the old one just stopped making ice, and my husband absolutely refuses to drink lukewarm water. He says it tastes like it's been sitting in a garden hose. I remember drinking out of the garden hose when I was a kid, playing outside until the streetlights came on. We'd play capture the flag with all the neighborhood kids, and my knees were always covered in grass stains. My mother hated trying to get those stains out of my jeans. She had this special soap she ordered from a catalog that smelled like pine needles. I love the smell of pine, actually. It reminds me of Christmas. We always got a real tree, right up until I was in high school and they bought one of those artificial ones with the lights already on it. It just wasn't the same. But, um... what was the question? Oh, weight. I haven't really been weighing myself, but my eating is definitely just... all over the place.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, underweight? No, I don't think I'm underweight, but like I mentioned, my eating is just... it's really all over the place. That's honestly what finally pushed me to come in and see you. Some weeks I'll barely even touch my dinner, and then other nights I'm just standing in the kitchen in the dark, eating crackers and sweets after everyone's gone to bed. It's so strange how quiet the house gets at night. You can hear every little creak. Our house was built in the 1950s, so the hardwood floors creak constantly, especially in the hallway upstairs. My husband tried to fix the squeakiest floorboard last year, but he ended up just making it worse, and then he dropped his hammer and it put this huge dent in the wood. He was so mad about that hammer. It belonged to his grandfather, who was a carpenter. He built these amazing wooden birdhouses that he painted in all these bright colors. We have one hanging in our backyard, but the squirrels keep trying to get into it to steal the birdseed. Squirrels are so clever, aren't they? They figure out every single bird feeder we buy. But, um... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, being underweight. No, I don't think so, just... the eating is all over the place.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I know we keep going over this, doctor, but no, I really don't consider myself underweight. It's just like I've been trying to tell you—what finally pushed me to make this appointment is that my eating has been completely unpredictable lately. Some weeks I barely touch whatever I make for dinner, and then other nights I'm just standing in the kitchen eating crackers and sweets after everyone else is in bed. 

I usually buy those sweets from the little corner market now because the big grocery store on 4th Street completely rearranged their aisles last month. It drives me absolutely crazy when they do that. I spent twenty minutes just looking for coffee filters last Tuesday. I always buy the unbleached ones, the brown ones, because my sister told me the white ones have bleach in them. She's completely obsessed with chemicals and toxins lately. She won't even use regular shampoo anymore, she just washes her hair with apple cider vinegar. She smells like a vinaigrette half the time, to be completely honest with you. We went out for lunch last week and I swear the waiter was wrinkling his nose when he took our order. We had salads, ironically. Mine had these wonderful little candied pecans on it, which were so sweet... but anyway, I'm sorry, you were asking about my weight again. Yeah, not underweight, just... my appetite is completely all over the place.

Restrict my eating on purpose? Um, no, I haven't been doing that. It's really just the unpredictability of it all, like I've been saying. I'm definitely not trying to starve myself or anything. Sometimes I'm just not hungry at all, and other times I can't stop eating. 

Like the other night, I was just eating those crackers, and I actually found this old jar of jam in the back of the fridge. It was boysenberry, I think. My aunt used to make homemade boysenberry jam every summer when we'd go stay with her at the lake. The lake was always so cold, even in July. We'd jump off the dock and the water would just take your breath away. My cousin actually lost his glasses in that lake once. We spent hours diving down trying to find them, but it was so murky at the bottom. He had to wear his prescription sunglasses for the rest of the trip, even inside, and he looked so ridiculous. He looked like a tiny little movie star sitting at the breakfast table eating his pancakes. My aunt made the best pancakes, they were always so fluffy. She said the secret was letting the batter rest for ten minutes before cooking them. I tried that last week, actually, but I think my baking powder was expired because they came out flat as paper. It's so frustrating when you go to all that trouble and it doesn't work out. But, um... what did you ask me? Oh, right, restricting my food. No, no, I'm not doing that on purpose at all. It's just the appetite changes.

She lived way out in the country, and the stars out there were just incredible. You really can't see stars like that anymore because of all the light pollution. I read this fascinating article the other day about how light pollution is completely messing up bird migrations. They were so red and juicy, I just sliced them up with some mozzarella and basil. Basil is so easy to grow, I should really start a little herb garden on the windowsill. I usually love the water, though. We used to go to the community pool all the time when I was a kid, and I'd just float on my back and look up at the clouds. You can really see shapes in them if you stare long enough. I saw one that looked exactly like a golden retriever once. We had a golden retriever named Buster when I was little, and he was just the sweetest thing. He used to sleep right at the foot of my bed and keep my feet warm in the winter. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi, Doctor. Well, um, what finally pushed me to come in is that my eating has been all over the place lately. Like, some weeks I barely touch dinner, you know? And then other nights, I just stand in the kitchen eating crackers and sweets after everyone’s gone to bed. It’s so quiet then. I used to love the quiet when I lived in my old apartment—it was on the third floor, and you could hear the traffic, but it was like a hum, almost like the ocean. I went to the beach once when I was in college, just drove down with some friends on a whim. We didn't even pack towels. Anyway, yeah, my appetite is just completely unpredictable right now.\n\nDoctor: When you eat those crackers and sweets at night, do you feel like you eat an unusually large amount of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say it's a ton of food all at once or anything like that. The sunlight hits the kitchen window perfectly in the mornings. But, um, yeah, sex drive is fine. No issues there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I've never done anything like that. I absolutely hate throwing up. No, I definitely don't make myself do that.\n\nDoctor: When you eat like that at night, do you feel out of control?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not out of control at all. You can hear the hum of the refrigerator. We had to get a new refrigerator last year because the old one just stopped making ice, and my husband absolutely refuses to drink lukewarm water. He says it tastes like it's been sitting in a garden hose. I remember drinking out of the garden hose when I was a kid, playing outside until the streetlights came on. We'd play capture the flag with all the neighborhood kids, and my knees were always covered in grass stains. My mother hated trying to get those stains out of my jeans. She had this special soap she ordered from a catalog that smelled like pine needles. I love the smell of pine, actually. It reminds me of Christmas. We always got a real tree, right up until I was in high school and they bought one of those artificial ones with the lights already on it. It just wasn't the same. But, um... what was the question? Oh, weight. I haven't really been weighing myself, but my eating is definitely just... all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I was saying before... what finally made me make an appointment is that my eating has just been completely all over the place. Some weeks I'll barely even touch my dinner, and then other nights I'm just standing in the kitchen, eating crackers and sweets long after everyone else is asleep. I love those little buttery crackers, the ones that come in the long sleeves? You have to be so careful opening them or they just crumble everywhere. My grandmother used to serve those with this sharp cheddar cheese whenever we went to visit her on Sundays. She lived in this old Victorian house that always smelled slightly like mothballs and peppermint. She kept these little hard peppermint candies in a crystal dish on the coffee table. I tried to find a dish just like it at an antique store last month, but everything was so expensive. The antique store was right next to this wonderful little bakery that makes the most incredible sourdough bread. The smell of fresh bread baking is just the best thing in the world, isn't it? It makes you feel so warm and cozy inside. But, um... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, losing weight. Well, like I said, my eating is just... it's all over the place right now.\n\nDoctor: Are you currently underweight?\n\nPatient: Um, underweight? No, I don't think I'm underweight, but like I mentioned, my eating is just... it's really all over the place. That's honestly what finally pushed me to come in and see you. Some weeks I'll barely even touch my dinner, and then other nights I'm just standing in the kitchen in the dark, eating crackers and sweets after everyone's gone to bed. It's so strange how quiet the house gets at night. You can hear every little creak. Our house was built in the 1950s, so the hardwood floors creak constantly, especially in the hallway upstairs. My husband tried to fix the squeakiest floorboard last year, but he ended up just making it worse, and then he dropped his hammer and it put this huge dent in the wood. He was so mad about that hammer. It belonged to his grandfather, who was a carpenter. He built these amazing wooden birdhouses that he painted in all these bright colors. We have one hanging in our backyard, but the squirrels keep trying to get into it to steal the birdseed. Squirrels are so clever, aren't they? They figure out every single bird feeder we buy. But, um... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, being underweight. No, I don't think so, just... the eating is all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you consider yourself to be underweight?\n\nPatient: I know we keep going over this, doctor, but no, I really don't consider myself underweight. It's just like I've been trying to tell you—what finally pushed me to make this appointment is that my eating has been completely unpredictable lately. Some weeks I barely touch whatever I make for dinner, and then other nights I'm just standing in the kitchen eating crackers and sweets after everyone else is in bed. \n\nI usually buy those sweets from the little corner market now because the big grocery store on 4th Street completely rearranged their aisles last month. It drives me absolutely crazy when they do that. I spent twenty minutes just looking for coffee filters last Tuesday. I always buy the unbleached ones, the brown ones, because my sister told me the white ones have bleach in them. She's completely obsessed with chemicals and toxins lately. She won't even use regular shampoo anymore, she just washes her hair with apple cider vinegar. She smells like a vinaigrette half the time, to be completely honest with you. We went out for lunch last week and I swear the waiter was wrinkling his nose when he took our order. We had salads, ironically. Mine had these wonderful little candied pecans on it, which were so sweet... but anyway, I'm sorry, you were asking about my weight again. Yeah, not underweight, just... my appetite is completely all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to restrict how much you eat on purpose?\n\nPatient: Restrict my eating on purpose? Um, no, I haven't been doing that. It's really just the unpredictability of it all, like I've been saying. I'm definitely not trying to starve myself or anything. Sometimes I'm just not hungry at all, and other times I can't stop eating. \n\nLike the other night, I was just eating those crackers, and I actually found this old jar of jam in the back of the fridge. It was boysenberry, I think. My aunt used to make homemade boysenberry jam every summer when we'd go stay with her at the lake. The lake was always so cold, even in July. We'd jump off the dock and the water would just take your breath away. My cousin actually lost his glasses in that lake once. We spent hours diving down trying to find them, but it was so murky at the bottom. He had to wear his prescription sunglasses for the rest of the trip, even inside, and he looked so ridiculous. He looked like a tiny little movie star sitting at the breakfast table eating his pancakes. My aunt made the best pancakes, they were always so fluffy. She said the secret was letting the batter rest for ten minutes before cooking them. I tried that last week, actually, but I think my baking powder was expired because they came out flat as paper. It's so frustrating when you go to all that trouble and it doesn't work out. But, um... what did you ask me? Oh, right, restricting my food. No, no, I'm not doing that on purpose at all. It's just the appetite changes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat an unusually large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Um, an unusually large amount of food? No, I don't think I do that. I mean, I eat the crackers and sweets at night, but it's not like a massive amount, just... grazing, I guess. I'll just stand there by the counter. The counter is granite, actually, we had it put in about five years ago. It's this beautiful dark green color with little flecks of gold in it. The contractor said it was imported from Brazil. He was such an interesting guy, the contractor. He used to be a professional surfer before he started doing kitchen remodels. He had all these crazy stories about surfing in Hawaii and Australia. He showed me a picture of this huge wave he caught once, it looked absolutely terrifying. I can't even imagine being out in the ocean like that, with sharks and jellyfish and who knows what else. I stepped on a jellyfish once when I was a kid at the beach in Florida. It stung so badly, my dad had to carry me all the way back to the car. We had this old station wagon with wood paneling on the sides, it got so hot in the summer because the air conditioning was broken. We used to roll all the windows down and just listen to the radio on long drives. But, um... sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, eating a lot of food. No, no, I don't think I have episodes like that, just the grazing at night.\n\nDoctor: Have your eating changes been causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Distress? Um, no, I wouldn't say it's causing me significant distress. It's really just a bit annoying, I guess. Like when I take the time to cook a whole meal and then I don't even want it. I made a chicken casserole on Monday and the oven timer went off, and I swear it has this really obnoxious beep now. It sounds exactly like one of those big delivery trucks backing up. \n\nWe get so many delivery trucks on my street lately because my neighbor, Mr. Henderson, is constantly ordering things online. He gets these huge boxes delivered almost every single day. I saw one on his porch Tuesday that was almost as tall as I am! I have no idea what he's buying in there. My sister thinks he's hoarding antique furniture, because she saw him carrying what looked like a carved wooden chair leg once, but she exaggerates everything. She bought an antique mirror at a flea market last year and swore it was haunted just because it had a weird smudge on the glass that wouldn't come off. We spent an hour scrubbing it with vinegar and old newspapers. Did you know newspaper is actually the best thing for cleaning glass? It doesn't leave any streaks at all. My grandfather taught me that. He used to read the Sunday paper cover to cover and then save all the pages in this big cardboard box in his garage... but, wait, I completely lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? Something about being upset? Oh, right, the eating. No, it's not really distressing me, just... you know, unpredictable.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

An unusually large amount of food? Um, no, I wouldn't say that. Like I said, I just kind of pick at things. Mostly those crackers, or maybe a slice of cheese. Vomit? Oh, heavens no. I couldn't do that. I actually have a really strong gag reflex, ever since I was a little girl. Afraid of gaining weight? No, not really. I mean, I don't think about it that much. I've always been pretty much the same size since my twenties. I still have a pair of jeans from college that I can fit into, actually. They're those classic Levi's, the ones with the real stiff denim? They don't make them like that anymore. Now everything has spandex in it so it stretches, which I guess is comfortable, but it just doesn't hold up the same way. 

I bought a pair of those stretchy pants last year when I was trying to get into yoga. I thought yoga would be good for my posture, you know? But the instructor kept talking about finding our "center" and breathing through our toes, which just didn't make any sense to me. How do you breathe through your toes? It's physically impossible. I much prefer walking. I try to walk around the neighborhood every evening, right around sunset. The sky gets these incredible shades of purple and orange, especially in the fall. I have this neighbor, Mrs. Higgins, who has a huge maple tree in her front yard, and the leaves turn this brilliant fire-engine red. She spends hours raking them into these perfect little piles, but then the wind just blows them all over the street anyway. It seems like such a futile effort, doesn't it? Like trying to sweep water uphill. I offered to help her once, but she said she likes the exercise. She's in her eighties and still does all her own yard work, which is amazing. I hope I have that much energy when I'm her age. She told me her secret is drinking a glass of warm water with lemon every morning. I tried it for a few days, but the lemon was just too acidic for my stomach. Oh, wait, sorry... weight? Right. No, I'm not afraid of gaining weight. It's just not something I worry about.

She won't even use regular shampoo anymore, she just washes her hair with apple cider vinegar. She smells like a vinaigrette half the time, to be completely honest with you. We went out for lunch last week and I swear the waiter was wrinkling his nose when he took our order. We had salads, ironically. Mine had these wonderful little candied pecans on it, which were so sweet... but anyway, I'm sorry, you were asking about my weight again. Yeah, not underweight, just... my appetite is completely all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to restrict how much you eat on purpose?\n\nPatient: Restrict my eating on purpose? Um, no, I haven't been doing that. It's really just the unpredictability of it all, like I've been saying. I'm definitely not trying to starve myself or anything. Sometimes I'm just not hungry at all, and other times I can't stop eating. \n\nLike the other night, I was just eating those crackers, and I actually found this old jar of jam in the back of the fridge. It was boysenberry, I think. My aunt used to make homemade boysenberry jam every summer when we'd go stay with her at the lake. The lake was always so cold, even in July. We'd jump off the dock and the water would just take your breath away. My cousin actually lost his glasses in that lake once. We spent hours diving down trying to find them, but it was so murky at the bottom. He had to wear his prescription sunglasses for the rest of the trip, even inside, and he looked so ridiculous. He looked like a tiny little movie star sitting at the breakfast table eating his pancakes. My aunt made the best pancakes, they were always so fluffy. She said the secret was letting the batter rest for ten minutes before cooking them. I tried that last week, actually, but I think my baking powder was expired because they came out flat as paper. It's so frustrating when you go to all that trouble and it doesn't work out. But, um... what did you ask me? Oh, right, restricting my food. No, no, I'm not doing that on purpose at all. It's just the appetite changes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat an unusually large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Um, an unusually large amount of food? No, I don't think I do that. I mean, I eat the crackers and sweets at night, but it's not like a massive amount, just... grazing, I guess. I'll just stand there by the counter. The counter is granite, actually, we had it put in about five years ago. It's this beautiful dark green color with little flecks of gold in it. The contractor said it was imported from Brazil. He was such an interesting guy, the contractor. He used to be a professional surfer before he started doing kitchen remodels. He had all these crazy stories about surfing in Hawaii and Australia. I ended up standing there looking at the birthday cards for like twenty minutes. \n\nMy niece's birthday is coming up, she's turning seven next week. She is absolutely obsessed with horses right now. No, I don't think I've ever felt like that. Like I said, I just graze a little. I usually stop when I run out of crackers in the sleeve, actually. I buy those water crackers, the ones that come in the little plastic trays inside the box? They're so delicate, half the time they're completely smashed by the time I get them home from the grocery store. \n\nThe grocery store has been a nightmare lately, honestly. They are re-paving the entire parking lot, so everyone has to park on this dirt patch behind the building. When it rained on Sunday, it turned into a complete mud pit. My favorite pair of sneakers got completely ruined. They were these bright blue ones I ordered online from a specialty running store in Oregon. I've never even been to Oregon, but I always wanted to go see the redwood trees. Wait, are redwoods in Oregon or California? I remember seeing a documentary about them on PBS, and they had this one tree you could actually drive a car through. Can you imagine how old a tree has to be to get that big? It's kind of sad, really, cutting a hole in something that's been alive for hundreds of years just so tourists can take a picture. I take a lot of pictures on my phone, mostly of the birds at my feeder, but... wait, I'm sorry. Did you ask about the crackers? Oh, feeling unable to stop eating. No, no, I can stop whenever I want.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat an unusually large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: An unusually large amount of food? Um, no, I wouldn't say that. Like I said, I just kind of pick at things. Mostly those crackers, or maybe a slice of cheese. I buy that sharp cheddar, the kind that comes in a block wrapped in black wax. It's so hard to cut, honestly. You really need a good cheese knife for it. I bought this little cheese knife set at a craft fair last summer, but I only ever use the one with the little prongs on the end. \n\nThe craft fair was down by the riverfront, and it was so incredibly hot that day. They had these big misting fans set up near the food trucks, but it barely helped. My friend Susan and I must have drank three huge lemonades each. Susan makes her own lemonade from scratch, actually. She has this giant lemon tree in her backyard that produces fruit almost all year round. She's always trying to give me bags of lemons. I don't even know what to do with them all. I tried making a lemon meringue pie once, but the meringue completely collapsed. It just looked like a sad, sticky puddle on top of the filling. Baking is so much harder than cooking, don't you think? It's like a science experiment where you have to measure everything perfectly or it's a total disaster. I was never very good at science in school, especially chemistry. I always felt like I was going to blow something up. But... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, eating a lot of food. No, no, I definitely don't eat huge amounts of food at once.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives after eating?\n\nPatient: Laxatives? Oh, no, I don't use those. I actually have a really sensitive stomach. I can't even handle spicy food anymore. I used to love going to this Thai place downtown, they made the best Pad Kee Mao, the drunken noodles? But they closed down a few years ago. I think the owner retired and moved back to Thailand, or maybe Florida. A lot of people retire to Florida, but I could never deal with that humidity. \n\nMy aunt lives near Tampa and she says she basically stays indoors from May to October because the air feels like a wet sponge. She has this huge screened-in porch, though, which is nice for sitting out in the evenings. She has these beautiful wicker chairs out there with floral cushions. Wicker is so hard to keep clean, though, dust gets into all those little woven grooves. You practically have to vacuum the furniture, which just seems ridiculous to me. Vacuuming is my least favorite chore, honestly. The cord always gets tangled around the legs of the coffee table. But... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, laxatives. Right. No, no, I've never used anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: Vomit? Oh, heavens no. I couldn't do that. I actually have a really strong gag reflex, ever since I was a little girl. I remember one time my mother tried to make me take this awful cherry-flavored cough syrup. It was thick, like molasses, and the color was just... unnatural. Like a neon red sign. \n\nSpeaking of neon signs, did you know that diner on 4th Street finally fixed theirs? The \"D\" had been burnt out for months, so it just said \"iner.\" I always thought that was kind of funny, but it drove my husband crazy. He's very particular about things being in working order. He spends every Saturday morning in the garage, tinkering with the lawnmower or organizing his tools. He has this pegboard where every single wrench has its own little outlined spot. It's almost like a museum display. I went to a modern art museum once, and they had this exhibit that was just a pile of hard candies wrapped in shiny cellophane. You were supposed to take one, and the pile would slowly disappear over time. I didn't take one, though. I'm always worried about the germs on things like that. So many people touching them... wait, I'm sorry. Did you ask about throwing up? Right, no, I definitely don't make myself do that. Never.\n\nDoctor: Has a doctor ever told you that you have a medical problem, like thyroid disease or diabetes, that could explain these eating changes?\n\nPatient: Medical problem? Um, no, no doctor has ever told me anything like that. I don't have thyroid issues or diabetes or anything. I did have to get my blood drawn a few months ago, just for a regular checkup, and I always get so nervous about the needle. The phlebotomist was very nice, though, she had this little butterfly clip in her hair that kept catching the light. \n\nIt reminded me of this stained glass window my grandmother used to have in her front hallway. When the sun hit it just right in the late afternoon, it would cast these beautiful colored shadows all over the wooden floor. She had these oak floors that she kept polished to a mirror shine. She got it at an estate sale in... oh, wait, what were you asking? Right, medical problems. No, no, my bloodwork was all completely normal. No thyroid things or diabetes.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid of gaining weight? No, not really. I mean, I don't think about it that much. I've always been pretty much the same size since my twenties. I still have a pair of jeans from college that I can fit into, actually. They're those classic Levi's, the ones with the real stiff denim? They don't make them like that anymore. Now everything has spandex in it so it stretches, which I guess is comfortable, but it just doesn't hold up the same way. \n\nI bought a pair of those stretchy pants last year when I was trying to get into yoga. I thought yoga would be good for my posture, you know? But the instructor kept talking about finding our \"center\" and breathing through our toes, which just didn't make any sense to me. How do you breathe through your toes? It's physically impossible. I much prefer walking. I try to walk around the neighborhood every evening, right around sunset. The sky gets these incredible shades of purple and orange, especially in the fall. She spends hours raking them into these perfect little piles, but then the wind just blows them all over the street anyway. It seems like such a futile effort, doesn't it? Like trying to sweep water uphill. I offered to help her once, but she said she likes the exercise. She's in her eighties and still does all her own yard work, which is amazing. I hope I have that much energy when I'm her age. She told me her secret is drinking a glass of warm water with lemon every morning. I tried it for a few days, but the lemon was just too acidic for my stomach. Oh, wait, sorry... weight? Right. No, I'm not afraid of gaining weight. It's just not something I worry about.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
A large amount of food all at once? No, no, I don't really do that. I just eat my regular meals. Although, speaking of food amounts, my sister recently bought one of those giant air fryers. Have you seen those? It takes up half her kitchen counter. She was trying to make an entire rotisserie chicken in it the other day, and the smell of rosemary just filled the whole house. 

I love the smell of rosemary. I tried to grow a little herb garden on my windowsill a few years ago, but my cat kept knocking the terracotta pots over. He’s an orange tabby, very mischievous. He actually managed to open a bottom cabinet once and got into a whole bag of flour. There were little white paw prints all over the linoleum floor! It took me forever to mop it all up. I had to use this special citrus cleaner, and the house smelled like a lemon orchard for a week. Which is funny, because I was just talking about lemons, wasn't I? With Mrs. Higgins. Wait, I'm sorry, what was your question again? Oh! Eating a lot in a short time. Right. No, I don't do that. I actually eat pretty slowly.

No issues there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I've never done anything like that. I absolutely hate throwing up. I think the last time I was even sick like that was when I got terrible motion sickness. My husband and I went on this ferry ride a few years back to visit this little island off the coast, and the water was just so choppy that day, all gray and angry looking. I usually love the water, though. We used to go to the community pool all the time when I was a kid, and I'd just float on my back and look up at the clouds. You can really see shapes in them if you stare long enough. I saw one that looked exactly like a golden retriever once. We had a golden retriever named Buster when I was little, and he was just the sweetest thing. He used to sleep right at the foot of my bed and keep my feet warm in the winter. Dogs are just so loyal, aren't they? They just want to be near you. But, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, throwing up. No, I definitely don't make myself do that.\n\nDoctor: When you eat like that at night, do you feel out of control?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not out of control at all. It's really just a little snack while I'm sitting there watching TV. I usually watch those old sitcom reruns on that classic channel, you know? Though sometimes they just play those crazy infomercials if it gets late enough. The other night there was one for this incredible blender that can supposedly turn whole apples and carrots into juice in like ten seconds. My mom used to have this awful, heavy green blender back in the 80s that sounded exactly like a jet engine taking off every time she made margaritas. She loved having the neighbors over for margaritas on the back patio during the summer. We had this massive oak tree in the backyard that gave the absolute best shade, at least until lightning struck a huge branch off it during a summer storm. The thunder was so loud it shook the whole house and rattled all her good china in the dining room cabinet. I was terrified of storms as a kid. But, um... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, being out of control with the food. No, no, it's nothing like that. I can stop eating whenever I want to.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a lot of weight recently, or are you currently underweight?\n\nPatient: Um, weight? Well, what finally pushed me to come in is that my eating has been all over the place lately. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and other nights I just stand in the kitchen eating crackers and sweets after everyone's gone to bed. The kitchen gets so quiet at night, you know? You can hear the hum of the refrigerator. We had to get a new refrigerator last year because the old one just stopped making ice, and my husband absolutely refuses to drink lukewarm water. He says it tastes like it's been sitting in a garden hose. I remember drinking out of the garden hose when I was a kid, playing outside until the streetlights came on. We'd play capture the flag with all the neighborhood kids, and my knees were always covered in grass stains. My mother hated trying to get those stains out of my jeans. She had this special soap she ordered from a catalog that smelled like pine needles. I love the smell of pine, actually. It reminds me of Christmas. We always got a real tree, right up until I was in high school and they bought one of those artificial ones with the lights already on it. It just wasn't the same. But, um... what was the question? Oh, weight. I haven't really been weighing myself, but my eating is definitely just... all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I was saying before... what finally made me make an appointment is that my eating has just been completely all over the place. Some weeks I'll barely even touch my dinner, and then other nights I'm just standing in the kitchen, eating crackers and sweets long after everyone else is asleep. I love those little buttery crackers, the ones that come in the long sleeves? You have to be so careful opening them or they just crumble everywhere. My grandmother used to serve those with this sharp cheddar cheese whenever we went to visit her on Sundays. She lived in this old Victorian house that always smelled slightly like mothballs and peppermint. She kept these little hard peppermint candies in a crystal dish on the coffee table. I tried to find a dish just like it at an antique store last month, but everything was so expensive. The antique store was right next to this wonderful little bakery that makes the most incredible sourdough bread. The smell of fresh bread baking is just the best thing in the world, isn't it? It makes you feel so warm and cozy inside. But, um... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, losing weight. Well, like I said, my eating is just... it's all over the place right now.\n\nDoctor: Are you currently underweight?\n\nPatient: Um, underweight? No, I don't think I'm underweight, but like I mentioned, my eating is just... it's really all over the place. That's honestly what finally pushed me to come in and see you. Some weeks I'll barely even touch my dinner, and then other nights I'm just standing in the kitchen in the dark, eating crackers and sweets after everyone's gone to bed. It's so strange how quiet the house gets at night. You can hear every little creak. Our house was built in the 1950s, so the hardwood floors creak constantly, especially in the hallway upstairs. My husband tried to fix the squeakiest floorboard last year, but he ended up just making it worse, and then he dropped his hammer and it put this huge dent in the wood. He was so mad about that hammer. It belonged to his grandfather, who was a carpenter. He built these amazing wooden birdhouses that he painted in all these bright colors. We have one hanging in our backyard, but the squirrels keep trying to get into it to steal the birdseed. Squirrels are so clever, aren't they? They figure out every single bird feeder we buy. But, um... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, being underweight. No, I don't think so, just... the eating is all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you consider yourself to be underweight?\n\nPatient: I know we keep going over this, doctor, but no, I really don't consider myself underweight. It's just like I've been trying to tell you—what finally pushed me to make this appointment is that my eating has been completely unpredictable lately. Some weeks I barely touch whatever I make for dinner, and then other nights I'm just standing in the kitchen eating crackers and sweets after everyone else is in bed. \n\nI usually buy those sweets from the little corner market now because the big grocery store on 4th Street completely rearranged their aisles last month. It drives me absolutely crazy when they do that. I spent twenty minutes just looking for coffee filters last Tuesday. I always buy the unbleached ones, the brown ones, because my sister told me the white ones have bleach in them. She's completely obsessed with chemicals and toxins lately. She won't even use regular shampoo anymore, she just washes her hair with apple cider vinegar. She smells like a vinaigrette half the time, to be completely honest with you. We went out for lunch last week and I swear the waiter was wrinkling his nose when he took our order. We had salads, ironically. Mine had these wonderful little candied pecans on it, which were so sweet... but anyway, I'm sorry, you were asking about my weight again. Yeah, not underweight, just... my appetite is completely all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to restrict how much you eat on purpose?\n\nPatient: Restrict my eating on purpose? Um, no, I haven't been doing that. It's really just the unpredictability of it all, like I've been saying. I'm definitely not trying to starve myself or anything. Sometimes I'm just not hungry at all, and other times I can't stop eating. \n\nLike the other night, I was just eating those crackers, and I actually found this old jar of jam in the back of the fridge. It was boysenberry, I think. My aunt used to make homemade boysenberry jam every summer when we'd go stay with her at the lake. The lake was always so cold, even in July. We'd jump off the dock and the water would just take your breath away. My cousin actually lost his glasses in that lake once. We spent hours diving down trying to find them, but it was so murky at the bottom. He had to wear his prescription sunglasses for the rest of the trip, even inside, and he looked so ridiculous. He looked like a tiny little movie star sitting at the breakfast table eating his pancakes. My aunt made the best pancakes, they were always so fluffy. She said the secret was letting the batter rest for ten minutes before cooking them. I tried that last week, actually, but I think my baking powder was expired because they came out flat as paper. It's so frustrating when you go to all that trouble and it doesn't work out. But, um... what did you ask me? Oh, right, restricting my food. No, no, I'm not doing that on purpose at all. It's just the appetite changes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat an unusually large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Um, an unusually large amount of food? No, I don't think I do that. I mean, I eat the crackers and sweets at night, but it's not like a massive amount, just... grazing, I guess. I'll just stand there by the counter. The counter is granite, actually, we had it put in about five years ago. It's this beautiful dark green color with little flecks of gold in it. The contractor said it was imported from Brazil. He was such an interesting guy, the contractor. He used to be a professional surfer before he started doing kitchen remodels. He had all these crazy stories about surfing in Hawaii and Australia. He showed me a picture of this huge wave he caught once, it looked absolutely terrifying. I can't even imagine being out in the ocean like that, with sharks and jellyfish and who knows what else. I stepped on a jellyfish once when I was a kid at the beach in Florida. It stung so badly, my dad had to carry me all the way back to the car. We had this old station wagon with wood paneling on the sides, it got so hot in the summer because the air conditioning was broken. We used to roll all the windows down and just listen to the radio on long drives. But, um... sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, eating a lot of food. No, no, I don't think I have episodes like that, just the grazing at night.\n\nDoctor: Have your eating changes been causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Distress? Um, no, I wouldn't say it's causing me significant distress. It's really just a bit annoying, I guess. Like when I take the time to cook a whole meal and then I don't even want it. I made a chicken casserole on Monday and the oven timer went off, and I swear it has this really obnoxious beep now. It sounds exactly like one of those big delivery trucks backing up. \n\nWe get so many delivery trucks on my street lately because my neighbor, Mr. Henderson, is constantly ordering things online. He gets these huge boxes delivered almost every single day. I saw one on his porch Tuesday that was almost as tall as I am! I have no idea what he's buying in there. My sister thinks he's hoarding antique furniture, because she saw him carrying what looked like a carved wooden chair leg once, but she exaggerates everything. She bought an antique mirror at a flea market last year and swore it was haunted just because it had a weird smudge on the glass that wouldn't come off. We spent an hour scrubbing it with vinegar and old newspapers. Did you know newspaper is actually the best thing for cleaning glass? It doesn't leave any streaks at all. My grandfather taught me that. He used to read the Sunday paper cover to cover and then save all the pages in this big cardboard box in his garage... but, wait, I completely lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? Something about being upset? Oh, right, the eating. No, it's not really distressing me, just... you know, unpredictable.\n\nDoctor: Have you started any new medications or drugs recently?\n\nPatient: New medications? Um, no, I haven't started anything new. Just the usual. I did go to the pharmacy the other day, though, just to get some toothpaste, and they completely rearranged the aisles. It's so confusing in there now. They put all the dental stuff right next to the greeting cards, which makes absolutely no sense to me. I ended up standing there looking at the birthday cards for like twenty minutes. \n\nMy niece's birthday is coming up, she's turning seven next week. She is absolutely obsessed with horses right now. We took her to this little petting zoo last month and she just wouldn't leave this one little pony alone. It was brown with this cute little white spot on its nose. It actually looked exactly like a dog I had when I was a teenager. His name was Buster, he was a beagle mix. Beagles have that really distinct howl, you know? He used to howl his head off every single time the mail carrier came up the front steps. Our mailman was this really nice guy, Gary, and he always kept dog treats in his front pocket just for Buster. He wore those funny little blue shorts even in the middle of winter. But, um, wait. Did you ask me about the pharmacy? Oh, right, new drugs. No, no, I haven't taken anything new.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when you felt unable to stop yourself from eating once you started?\n\nPatient: Unable to stop? No, I don't think I've ever felt like that. Like I said, I just graze a little. I usually stop when I run out of crackers in the sleeve, actually. I buy those water crackers, the ones that come in the little plastic trays inside the box? They're so delicate, half the time they're completely smashed by the time I get them home from the grocery store. \n\nThe grocery store has been a nightmare lately, honestly. They are re-paving the entire parking lot, so everyone has to park on this dirt patch behind the building. When it rained on Sunday, it turned into a complete mud pit. My favorite pair of sneakers got completely ruined. They were these bright blue ones I ordered online from a specialty running store in Oregon. I've never even been to Oregon, but I always wanted to go see the redwood trees. Wait, are redwoods in Oregon or California? I remember seeing a documentary about them on PBS, and they had this one tree you could actually drive a car through. Can you imagine how old a tree has to be to get that big? It's kind of sad, really, cutting a hole in something that's been alive for hundreds of years just so tourists can take a picture. I take a lot of pictures on my phone, mostly of the birds at my feeder, but... wait, I'm sorry. Did you ask about the crackers? Oh, feeling unable to stop eating. No, no, I can stop whenever I want.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat an unusually large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: An unusually large amount of food? Um, no, I wouldn't say that. Like I said, I just kind of pick at things. Mostly those crackers, or maybe a slice of cheese. I buy that sharp cheddar, the kind that comes in a block wrapped in black wax. It's so hard to cut, honestly. You really need a good cheese knife for it. I bought this little cheese knife set at a craft fair last summer, but I only ever use the one with the little prongs on the end. \n\nThe craft fair was down by the riverfront, and it was so incredibly hot that day. They had these big misting fans set up near the food trucks, but it barely helped. My friend Susan and I must have drank three huge lemonades each. Susan makes her own lemonade from scratch, actually. She has this giant lemon tree in her backyard that produces fruit almost all year round. She's always trying to give me bags of lemons. I don't even know what to do with them all. I tried making a lemon meringue pie once, but the meringue completely collapsed. It just looked like a sad, sticky puddle on top of the filling. Baking is so much harder than cooking, don't you think? It's like a science experiment where you have to measure everything perfectly or it's a total disaster. I was never very good at science in school, especially chemistry. I always felt like I was going to blow something up. But... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, eating a lot of food. No, no, I definitely don't eat huge amounts of food at once.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives after eating?\n\nPatient: Laxatives? Oh, no, I don't use those. I actually have a really sensitive stomach. I can't even handle spicy food anymore. I used to love going to this Thai place downtown, they made the best Pad Kee Mao, the drunken noodles? But they closed down a few years ago. I think the owner retired and moved back to Thailand, or maybe Florida. A lot of people retire to Florida, but I could never deal with that humidity. \n\nMy aunt lives near Tampa and she says she basically stays indoors from May to October because the air feels like a wet sponge. She has this huge screened-in porch, though, which is nice for sitting out in the evenings. She has these beautiful wicker chairs out there with floral cushions. Wicker is so hard to keep clean, though, dust gets into all those little woven grooves. You practically have to vacuum the furniture, which just seems ridiculous to me. I actually have a really strong gag reflex, ever since I was a little girl. I remember one time my mother tried to make me take this awful cherry-flavored cough syrup. It was thick, like molasses, and the color was just... unnatural. Like a neon red sign. \n\nSpeaking of neon signs, did you know that diner on 4th Street finally fixed theirs? The \"D\" had been burnt out for months, so it just said \"iner.\" I always thought that was kind of funny, but it drove my husband crazy. He's very particular about things being in working order. He spends every Saturday morning in the garage, tinkering with the lawnmower or organizing his tools. He has this pegboard where every single wrench has its own little outlined spot. It's almost like a museum display. I went to a modern art museum once, and they had this exhibit that was just a pile of hard candies wrapped in shiny cellophane. You were supposed to take one, and the pile would slowly disappear over time. I didn't take one, though. I'm always worried about the germs on things like that. When the sun hit it just right in the late afternoon, it would cast these beautiful colored shadows all over the wooden floor. She had these oak floors that she kept polished to a mirror shine. It was made of brass and had these intricate little lions carved into the base. He knocked it over and dented it, and my grandmother was so upset. She loved that umbrella stand. She got it at an estate sale in... oh, wait, what were you asking? Right, medical problems. No, no, my bloodwork was all completely normal. No thyroid things or diabetes.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid of gaining weight? No, not really. I mean, I don't think about it that much. I've always been pretty much the same size since my twenties. I still have a pair of jeans from college that I can fit into, actually. They're those classic Levi's, the ones with the real stiff denim? They don't make them like that anymore. Now everything has spandex in it so it stretches, which I guess is comfortable, but it just doesn't hold up the same way. \n\nI bought a pair of those stretchy pants last year when I was trying to get into yoga. I thought yoga would be good for my posture, you know? But the instructor kept talking about finding our \"center\" and breathing through our toes, which just didn't make any sense to me. How do you breathe through your toes? It's physically impossible. I much prefer walking. I try to walk around the neighborhood every evening, right around sunset. The sky gets these incredible shades of purple and orange, especially in the fall. I have this neighbor, Mrs. Higgins, who has a huge maple tree in her front yard, and the leaves turn this brilliant fire-engine red. She spends hours raking them into these perfect little piles, but then the wind just blows them all over the street anyway. It seems like such a futile effort, doesn't it? Like trying to sweep water uphill. I offered to help her once, but she said she likes the exercise. She's in her eighties and still does all her own yard work, which is amazing. I hope I have that much energy when I'm her age. She told me her secret is drinking a glass of warm water with lemon every morning. I tried it for a few days, but the lemon was just too acidic for my stomach. Oh, wait, sorry... weight? Right. No, I'm not afraid of gaining weight. It's just not something I worry about.\n\nDoctor: In the past few months, have you had episodes where you ate a very large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: A large amount of food all at once? No, no, I don't really do that. I just eat my regular meals. Although, speaking of food amounts, my sister recently bought one of those giant air fryers. Have you seen those? It takes up half her kitchen counter. She was trying to make an entire rotisserie chicken in it the other day, and the smell of rosemary just filled the whole house. \n\nI love the smell of rosemary. I tried to grow a little herb garden on my windowsill a few years ago, but my cat kept knocking the terracotta pots over. He’s an orange tabby, very mischievous. He actually managed to open a bottom cabinet once and got into a whole bag of flour. There were little white paw prints all over the linoleum floor! It took me forever to mop it all up. I had to use this special citrus cleaner, and the house smelled like a lemon orchard for a week. Which is funny, because I was just talking about lemons, wasn't I? With Mrs. Higgins. Wait, I'm sorry, what was your question again? Oh! Eating a lot in a short time. Right. No, I don't do that. I actually eat pretty slowly."
